का . प्रा . 1964 : - केन्द्रीय सरकार ने , होयसा धारक क्षेत्र ( जैन और विकास ) अधिनियम , 1957 ( 1937 का 20 ) 
की धारा 7 की उप -धारा ( 1 ) के अधीन आरी की गई और भारत के राजपत्र , भाग 2, खंड 3, उप -खंड ( ii ) के पृष्ठ 3576 
से 3582 पर प्रकाशित भारत सरकार के कोषला मंत्रालय की अधिसूचना का . आ. स 2325 तारीख 29 अगस्त , 1991 द्वारा 
उम अधिसूचना से संलग्न अनुसूची में योगत भूमि का अजैन करने के अपने प्राशय मी सचना ही थी : 


और केन्द्रीय सरकार की जानकारी में यह बात लाई गई है कि राजपन्न में उक्त मधिसूचना के प्रकाशन मे मुद्रण प्रगति 
की कुछ त्रुटियां पाई गई है । 


अतः, अब , केन्द्रीय सरकार , उक्त प्राधिसुचना की धारा 7 को उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों और इस निमित अन्य 
सामर्यकारी शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए उन अधिसूचना से संलग्न अनुसूची में निम्नलिखिन संशोधन करती है : -- -

का आ 1965--- - केन्द्रीय सरकार , सरकारी स्थान 
( अप्राधिकृत अधिभोगियो की बेदखली ) अधिनियम , 1971 
( 1971 का 40 ) की धारा 3 द्वारा प्रदस शक्तियो का 
प्रयोग करते हा और खान मत्रालय की अधिसूचना का आ 
म 1584 तारीख 5 जून , 1989 को उन बातो के मिनाए 
अधिकात करते हुए जिन्हे ऐसे अधिक्रमण मे पहले किया गया 
है या करने का लोप किया गया है , नीचे सारणी के स्तम्भ 
( 1 ) मे वणित अधिकारी को , जो मरकार के गजपनित 
अधिकारी की समतुल्य पक्ति का अधिकारी है , उक्त अधि 
नियम के प्रयोजनो के लिए सपदा अधिकारी नियुक्त करती है 
और आग निदेश देती है कि उक्त अधिकारी उक्त मारणी के 
स्तम्भ ( 2 ) की तत्स्थानी प्रविष्टि में विनिर्दिष्ट सरकारी 
स्थानी की बाबत अपनी अधिकारिता की स्थानीय सीमाओ 
के भीतर उक्त अधिनियम द्वारा या उसके अधीन मपदा 

नारी को प्रदत्त शक्तिया का प्रशंग और उस पर 
अधिरोपित कलेव्यो का पालन करगा ।

workman , have no substanice .

tioned eariier, has not mentioned about pemanent or casual

that since the employee dying in barness was a casual work

ciaim for any permaneat employment. A part from the

legaliy or beyond any reasonable doubt, as neither the Union

11 . Mr. Murthy of course , in support of his submissions

has not been established , so 17. Ikut required to coosider 
these judgements or the applicability of them in this case . . 
I should further 60 noted that the emplovers also , in view

ducad certain records, torox copies where of, are kepi with

workinan .

SUPPLEMENTARY AWARD 
As per Order No. L -42011/ 81- D . II ( B ) dated 5 - 3 - 1987 the 
Government of India have referied this Industıial dispute 
for adjudicat on to this 111brinal. 

2 . Altogether five issues have been referred for adjudica 
tion . After considering the con entions advanced by both 
sides and evidence adduced before this Tribural, by arvard 
dated 12 12. 1988 this Tribunal allowed issue numbers 2 to 4 
in favour of the workmen and negarived the claim under 
ssue No. 1. Both sides challeaged the award passed by this 
Tribunal before Central Administrative Tribunal Ernakulam 
(CAT for short ) as per OA No. 403 /89 and OA No. 94 / 90 . 
The CAT as per decision dated 31. 8 1990 quashed the award 
of this Tribunal so far as it relates to issue No . I and a 
portion of issue No . III with regard to claim of uniform 
and directed th s Tribunal to consider these claims a fresh . 
That is how this matter is now before this Trihunal. 
3. I shall summarise belo :v issues numbers I and III. 
I. “Whether the Director of the Government of India 

Production Centres , Ettunanoor was justified in 
fixing 36 Helper Mistries recruited n 1971 - 72 as 
mistrſes at minjm ın of the scales , 110 - 155 after 
one year of their service 12 the lower scale , or 
whether they should have been given a higher start 
in the scale as per their appointment orders ? If so , 

what should be ?" 
III . " Whether the demand of Helpers under the Director 

Government of India Production Centre , Ettuma 
noor for quasi- permanency , confirmation , provision 
of un forms, gratuity , pension and other service 
benefits applicable to regular empioyees is legal 
and justified ? If so , to what extent and subject to 
what conditions." 


4 . The post of mistries and helpers ire two un ts of the 
cadre and Director of Produc ion Centre was the appointing 
authority . It is not disputeal that the appointment was in a 
non - gazetted establishment. It is also not ( isputed that as 
per memorandum No. 25 SSI ( C ) ( 13 ) 62 dated 18 - 9 - 1962 in 
Ext. M10 Award of Industiiri Tribunal Madras and M8 
judgment of the Hgh Court of Kerala and ir. the argument 
notes filed this Tricunal on behalf of management, the Presi 
dent of India delegated th : powers ti make aprointments 10 
Class IV post and Class III p .ist where the scale of pay does 
not exceed Rs. 300 - PM The Director of Production Centre 
as empowered as stated above offered arpointment to the 
candidates who were called for interviow as is evident from 
Ext . W2. The scale of nay of Rs. 75 - 95 is clearly stated . 
It was also offered to yne is higher scale of Rs 110 - 155 after 
seeing the performance of few morths . Ext W2 also refers 
the terms and cond tioris of appointpient. I his offer Wos 
accepted by all the workers wilo are now before this 
Tribunal. Ext M1 is one of the undertaking of accep ance 
submitted by Sri. K . K . Madhavan Nair. All the 36 helper 
mistries accepted the offer of appointment and reported for 
duty . It is true that the vorkers were called for interview 
for the post of mistry in the pay scale of Rs. 110 - 155 as is evi 
dent from Ext. W1. But he offer of apno ntment was to the 
post of helper mistry in the pay scale of Rs. 75- 95 which 
was accepted by the 36 persons and reported for city ard 
continued there without raisirg any objec ion . Since there 
was an ofler and acceptance and also that there was no 
legal defect in the order of appointment, it is not upon to 
the workmen to contend that the Director had no power 
to make such appointment. If the Director had no power to 
a point helper mistries as conterded by the union , the result 
vould he that the workers were not validly appointed to any 
rost. The Director in exercise of the power delegated to him 
for appointment of Class ! ! I and Class IV rost appointed 
he per mistries and watched their performance and then 
appointed them as mistries . It was specifically stated in the 
order of appointment that h eher start woult be given and 
after one year all 36 workinen were given the pay scale of 
Rs. 110 - 155 . It may be reviled that on behalf of 34 inistries 
their association had adjudicved the same matter before the 
Industrial Tribunal Madras in I. D 3174 and I D . 27174 and 
that Tribunal rejected the claim of the workers. That find 
ing was confirmed by the High Court of Kerala in OP -4228 
75 and writ appeal No. 83 77. 


Issue No. 1 . The case of lc union is that 36 permanent 
posts of mistries in the scale of Rs. 110 - 155 was lving vacant 
at Government of India Production Centre , Ettumanoor 
( Production Centre for Short ) which was created by Gov . 
ernment of India . The Director of Production Centre (The 
Director for short ) requested the Employment Exchange 
specifying the post , qualification etc . and the exchange spon 
sored various candidates and 35 persons were selected as 
mistries ( Class - III ) , Group C post. But offered a lower post 
of helper mistry ( Class - IV ) Group D in the scale of 
Rs. 75- 95 . Further case is that there was an undertaking to 
give a higher start in the scale of Rs. 110 - 155 after seeing 
their performance for a few months. According to the union 
the appointment orders issued are not in accordance with 
the instructions of the Government of India These persons 
continued for one year ard they wire appointed in the scale 
of 110 - 155. The case of the union is that the D rector is 
not competent to create a post , ir - designation and de - grading 
of these workers as helper mistry (Class - IV ) is unjust and 


5 . The appa ntment of helper mistries is an appointment 
of Class IV post in the scale of pay below Rs. 300 P .M . 
and these are posts in a lower cadre than the mistries in 
Class III post. Both hese cadros are in the establishment . 
Therefore there cannot be any violation of delecation of 
financial powers. The post of he rers is in existence and 
therefore the appointment of he rer mistries does not amount 
to creation of a post. The rersons were selected as hel 
pers for apnointing them in the post of mistries in future 
and they were so appointed alao But it does not amount to 
redesignation or de- grading of ho post. There is no altera 
tion in the condition of recruitinent or service . There was 
no lowering of the qualification also . So there s no viointinn 
of O . M . No F28 ( 1 ) EF1 88 dated 12 - 7 - 1958. These persons 
were pot fixed in a lower scale but offercd the helpers post 


3190 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 25 , 1992 / SRAVANA 3 , 1914 


[PART IISec . 3 ( 1) ] 


with undertaking to give higher start watching their perfor 
mance . Subsequently they were g ven higher start and the 
post of mistries. So the question of lowering the scale of pay 
does not arise . 


any statute or rules as againsi the documentary evidence of 
management mentioned above , the present claim is only to 
be rejected . I do so . 


6 . The terms and conditions of appointment were also not 
changed . After intervicw these workers were kept under senior 
mistries for one year 4S under study and those who were 
found satisfactory were regularised as spoken to by MW2. 
MW2 has further deposed that these workers were doing 
operations of machines and by putting them as helper mis 
tries it was intended to cnable thrin to attain the capacity 
to handle the machines independerly . After watching their 
performance for one year they were regularised as inistries . 
This arrangement was worked out exercising the admin 
istrative discretion availab e to the appointing authority under 
the existing rules . Therefore the question ot violating Em 
ployment Exchange Act , 1959 or any other rules does not 
also arise . Now the contention that in Exts . W3 and W4 
these persons are described as mistries and therefore that 
proves that these persons are working as mnistries from the 
date of appointment. But that was clari cd by MW2 who 
issued Exts . W3 and W4 that it wiis a mistake and the pur 
pose of their appointment was to learn job of mistries. 
Therefore Exts . W3 and W4 does nut prove that these 
workers were working is m stries from the date of appoint . 
ment. 

7 . From the above discussion it is clear that there is no 
merit in the contentions advanced by the union under issue 
No. I There is no illegality , injustice or violation of the 
principles of natural justice in the process of selection and 
appointment of these workmen as mistries . These workmen 
were appointed by their written consent only . Hay ng ac 
cepted the offer of appointment without any objection what 
so -ever and joined duty and continued there , these workers 
cannot now turn round and contend thai process of selec 
tion was illegal and the manageinent has violated rules and 
circulars . There are no justifiable reasons to hold that the 
Director Production Centre has violated any rules or direc 
tions of the Government of India . The result is that the 
workers are not entitled to any relief as claimed under 
issue No. 1. 

8 . The next claim of the union is regarding uniform and 
washing allowance which is included under issue No. III. 
According to the union the persons working n industrial 
establishment are provided with two sets of uniform in an 
year and are granted with washing allowance . Since tho 
workers in this case are appointed in Class IV category, they 
are entitled to get two sets of uniform and washing allow 
ance according to the union as per Annexure - III to Gov 
ornment of India , Ministry of Home Affairs , OM No. 5 ,4158 
Public- II dated 27 - 2 - 1962 . It is opposed by the manage nent 
mainly on the ground that these workmen are not eligible 
for uniform as per " Ilani BJOK of Uniform of Gr. Cand 
D Employees ” since issue of un forms to Government of 
India employees is regulated by this book issued by Gov 
ernment of India . According to the management skillent 
workers and helpers are eligible for Aprons as per Ext. M3 
letter dated 6 -6 - 1985 . The clajm of uniform has already been 
considered and rejected by the Industrial Tribunal. Madrus 
in I . D . 374 and 27 74 as s cvident from Ext . M10 award . 
The learned counsel for the union argued that these workers 
are entitled to get uniform as per Annexure II% to Govern 
ment of India circular dated 27- 2 - 1972 mentioned above . 
But it is evident from pages 52 : nd 53 of Hand Book of 
uniforms mentioned above the workers in question are not 
included in the category of employees who are elig ble for 
uniforms in Class III and Cliss IV employees . Further , as 
per Ext. M3 order they are eligible to get Aprons. No evi 
dence is forthcoming in support of the claim of urifoim . 
WW1 has stated that the workers are eligjhle for uniform 
and that they are entitled to get un form allowance . It is 
also stated that workers handling machines and chemicals 
are eligible for uniforms as yer rules. But no such miles as 
stated in the claim statement were brought to the notice of 
this Tribunal. There is also no evidence to show that similar 
category of workers in other establ shments are getting uni 
forms. WW1 has not spoken a word about any establish 
ment supplying uniform to similarly placed emiployees . 
MW2 has categorically denied the claim of the workmen 
which is supported by the Hand Book of uniforms and Ext. 
M3 order. Since the union failed to establish the elig tility 
of the workers for uniform and washing allowance under 


9. In view of the above discussion , I hold that the claim 
of the workers under issue Io. I and the clam of uniform 
and washing allowance under issue No. III are devoid of 
merit and unsustainable . The workers are therefore not en 
titied to any relief . 
An award is passed accordingly. 

C . N . SASIDHARAN , Industrial Tritunal

" Whether the claim of Bank of India Employecs Cong

Cash - Cur - Accounts Clerk in Ajwan Branch per 
formed duties of Agricultural Assistant from April 
84 to Aprit 89 and is therefore entitled to special 
allowance attached to the post of Agricultural Assis 
tant la justified ? If so to what relief the workman 

is entitled 7" 
2 . The case of the concorned workman , as disclosed in 
the written statement submitted on bis behalf by the spon 
soring union, Bank of India Employees Congress (Blhar 
State ), details apart, is as follows : 

Suresh Chandra Prasad , Cash -cum -Accounts Clerk was 
omployed at Ajwan Branch of tho Bank of India perma 
nently in the year 1984 . The post of Agricultural Assistant 
was created at Ajwan Branch in 1984 . Shri Prased had off 
ciated against the permanent post of Agricultural Assistant 
from April, 1984 till 1989 when a permanent- incumbent 
joined there . Clause 9 . 11 ( 2 ) of the 1st Bipartite Settlement

" If a workman other than subordinate glaff officiates 

in a post in a high cadro for a period of 10 days 
of more , he shall be paid an officiating allowanco 

for the period for which he officiates." 
Shri Prasad porformed to duties of Agricultural Assistant in

wan Branch has functioned only for a period of 4 days 
to moot the needs of the farmers since April , 1984 to April ,

Those works are of general nature which are performed by 
a clork of the Bank . The duties of Agricultural Assistants ato

" Duties of Agricultural Assistants

AWARD 
By Order No. L -20012 ( 3 ) 18y -1 R (Coal- I) , dated , tho 
21st August, 1989 , the Central Government in the Ministry 
of Laboui , ilas, in exercise of the powers conferred by clause 
(a ) of sub -section ( 1 ) and sub -section (2 - A ) of s- ct.on 10 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 , referred the following 
dispute for adjudication to this Tribunal : 
" Whether the action of the management of Jitpur 

Colliery of Ms. Indian Iron & Steel Co . Ltd ., P . O . 
Jitpur, Dist. Dhanbad in dismissing Shri Nageswar 
Prasad Sinha , P . No . 04306 vide letter No. NI| 
Tesn -Ser87 283 dated 20 - 6 - 87 is justified ? If not, 

1o what relief the concerned workman is cntitled ? " 
2 . The case of the management of Jitpur Colliery of 
Ms. Indian Iron & Steel Co . Ltd ., Dhanbad , as disclosed in 
the written statement details apart , is as follows : 

Nageshwar Prasad Sidha, the concerned workman , was 
cmployed previously as a Tino Section Clerk at Jiipur Col 
liery , Information relating to certain Acts of misconduct 
allegedly committed by him relating to the period from 
April, 1983 to May , 1984 reached the management, Upon 
consideration of the matter , tho concorned workopen was 
issuçd with a charge - sheet dated 11 - 2 - 1986 . He submitted 
his interim reply dated 7 - 3 - 86 denying tho charges and 
roquested for supply of copies of documents referred to in 
the charge- sheet issued to him . The management joformed 
him by its lotter dated 3 - 4 - 86 that the documents referred 
to in the charge -shect would be made available during the 
enquiry . By the same letter the managemcat ordered 
domestic cuquiry and appointed Shri Mohit Mukherjee, 
Manager (Personnel), Ms. IISCO collieries as Enquiry 
Oficer to conduct an enquiry into the charges levelled against 
him . The Enquiry Officer held the enquiry after due notice 
to the concerned workman who participated in the enguiry . 
All thh documents relied on by the management were pro 
duced in the enquiry and the same were shown to him and 
he put his signature thereon . The managemont examired 
Shri R . Mohan as first witness and the concerned workman 
cross examined him partly with the assistance of his co -worker 
Bct thereafter he did not turn up for completion of cross- exa 
mn pation of R . Mohan in spite of repeated chances given to 
bim . Smeeth did not turn up in the cnquiry , the next witness of 
the maz gement , K . Prasad was examined ex - parte in his 
abscace . Theroafter the Enquiry Officer submitted his report 
of enuqiry dated 25-4 - 87. In the report the Erquiry Officer 
gave his findings to the effect that on the basis of evidence 
produced by the management in the enquiry the charges 
framed against the concerned workman were fully establ shed . 
Thereafter the matter was considered by the Manager , Nooni 
dih -Jitpur Colllory who accepted the findings of the Enquiry 
Officer and came to the conclusion that this wag # fit case 
for dismissal of the concerned workman from service , 
Thereafter the matter was also considered by the Superin on 
dcat (J) . Noonidih - Jitpur Collicry ; be also agreed with the 
views of the Manager, Noonidih - Jitpur Colliery. The case 
filo was subsequently put up before the Chief Mining 
Engincer and he also accepted the findings of the Enquiry 
Officer and passed an order to the effect that the concerned 
wosknan should be dismissed from service. Accordingly , ho 
was dismissed from service by the management vide letter 
dated 20 -6 - 87 which was signed by the Manager , Noonidih 
Jitpur Colliery . In the circumstances , the management has 
prayed that the action taken by the management in dismiss 
ing the corcerned workman is fully justified . 

3 . The case of the concerned workman , Ag appearing 
from the written statement submitted on his behalt by Janta 
Mivdoor Sangh , briefly stated , is as follows : 

Nageshwar Prasad Siha, the concerned workman , was 
a permanent workman of Jitpur Colliery of Ms. IISCO . 
He was charge-sheeted by the management by memorandum 
dated 11 - 2 - 86 alongwith four Annoxurts , nomely , Articles 
of charge (Annexure - I), Statement of imputation of mirc yn . 
dict or misbehaviour in support of the Articles of charge 
(Annexure- II), a list of documents numbering 33 under 17 
heads (Annexure -JJI ) and a list of witnęgges numbering 14 
(Annexufc - IV ). The workman concerned submitted an 
interim reply to the charge - sheet derying tlie charges and 
prayed for copies of documents listed in the annexure- It 
for exam nation of the same for submitting a comprehensive 
reply . The management did not, how wer , consider it neces 
sary to supply the coples of the documents for a comprehen . 


sive reply to the charge -sheet and found that the interim 
reply was unsatisfactory . A domestic enquiry was held com , 
mercing from 2 - 8 -86 during which the prioc .ples of natural 
Justice were grussly violated . The then Acting Manager, 
upon relying on the preverse and prcjudicial findings of tho 
Enquiry Officer, awarded the concerned workmen the 
punishment of dismissal from service. By denying to fur 
mush the workman with copios of documents and withholding 
tho Sume, the management virtually deprived him of an 
opportunity to preparc his defence. It has been alleged 
that thc disciplinary action has been taken by the manago 
ment vindictively as the workman had preferred an applica 
tion before the Presiding Officer, the thon Contral Govt. 
Industrial Tnbunal- Cum -Labour Court (No. 3 ) . Dhanbad , 
under Section 33- C ( 2 ) of the Industrial Disputes Act clain 
ing subsistence allowance for the period of suspension . 
There was no positive evidence against him , even then the 
management framed the charge against him on the basis of 
surmises and conjectures . At the preliminary stage the 
workman was kept aware of any case of ¢x _ css payment 
on account of overtime wages and the management with & 
malatide motivc hag placed the whole burden on him in order 
to make him a scape- goat. The allegation of manipulation 
on the inechanised attendance sheet was not proved satis 
factorily during domestic enquiry . Although 14 witnesses 
Here listed to be examined by the managemcnt, only two 
witnesses were examined and the rest wero withdrawn by 
the management in order to be on the safe side . The evi 
deuce of R . Mohan , Manager (Personnel) discloses the sys 
len of work at the time office and with regard to charge 
of actual manipulation, his ovidence was vaguc and indirect. 
The evidence of R Prasad , Head Timekeeper , is motivated 
in order to save his own skin . No motive of personal gain 
was alleged against the workman in the articles of charge . 
Anyway , the workman has been awarded the punishment of 
dismissal on indirect, vague and meegro evidence and there 
fore , the actioo of the management in dismissing him from 
service with effect from 20 -6 -87 is not justified . In the 
circumstances , the union has asserted that the action of 
the management in dismissing the concerned workman from 
service is unjustified and has prayed that the management 
be directed to reinstate bim in sorvice with full back wages . 

4 . In rejoinder to the written statement of the sponsoring 
union , the management has stood its ground and asserted 
that the concerned workman wag guilty of the chargo 
levelled against him . It has been denied that tho Andings 
of the Enquiry Officer arç perverse Or that they can bo 
assailed on any ground whatsocver. It is for the manage 
mert to decide to examine all the witnesses or such witness 
25 may be considered necessary to bring the charge home to 
the concerned workman . It is not necessary that all the 
witnesses listed should be examined . In other words , tho 
rejoinder of the management is a total rebuttal of statements 
of facts and allegation as lovelled in the written statement 
of the sponsoring linion. 

$ . In rejoinder to the written statement of the manago 
ment the sponsoring Union has reiterated its own case . 

6 . At the instance of the manugement the fairness and 
propriety of the domestic enquiry was considered as prelimi. 
nary issue . In tho course of hearing the preliminary issue , 
tho management adduccd in evidence a glicet of documents 
which were marked exhibits . No oral evidence , however , 
was not adduced by the management, 

On the other hand , the sponsoring union cxamined the 
concerned workman , Nageswar Prasad Sinha and lald in 
evidence only one item of docuncat which was marked 
Ext. W - 1 , 

At the conclusion of hearing on preliminary issue Shri 
D . Mukherjee , learned Advocate for the concerned workman , 
that the domestic enquiry was held fairly and properly . 
Accordingly , it was held that the domestic enquiry was held 
fairly and properly . Thereafter the case was heard op merits. 

7 . Nageshwar Prasad Sinha , tho concerned workman , has 
disclosed in his evidence before this Tribunal that he was 
appointed in Jitpur Colliery of Ms. Indian Tron & Steel 
Co . Ltd , in 1965 as Sanitary Attendant in the hospital. This 
statement of fact has not been disputed by the monagement, 
It appears that on 11- 2 -86 when the chargesheet (Ext. M - 1 ) 
was issued to the concerned workman he was designated 
as Çantech Incharge but working as Celik io Personnel

From the evidence of these two witnesses it appears that 
niechan sed attendance sheets used to be prepared on the 
basis of attendance registers and other registers and these 
mechanised attendance sheets used to be sent to D . P . C . 
at Purppur for billing ol wages . [1 is the case of the 
management that the concerned workman used lo post 
intlated figures in respect cf somo daily rated workmen in 
the mechaniscd attendance sheet and D . P . C . used to bill 
wage - sheets on the basis of such mechanised attendance 
sheet and in the process excess payments were made to 
these workmen and the concerned workman adopted tho 
subterfuge of crasing out the inflated figures in inochanised 
attendance sheets and replaced the same by actual figure . 
In order to prove this fact that tho D . P . C . billed wage 
sheet as per mechanised stiendance sheet , it was necessary 
for the management to oxamine witness from the D . P . C . 
in order to sustain the fact that thic D . P . C . billed the 
amount as per mechanised attendance shect. It appears 
that one end of the rope was at Noonodih - Jitpur colliery 
and the other and at D . P . C . at Burnpur. The mechani. ed 
attendance was used to be prepared at Noonolib - Jitpur 
coll cry while billing of wage as per mechanised attendance 
sheet was done by D . P . C . at Burnpur . Hence , in my view , 
a compotent person from D . P . C . should have been ex 
mined by the management in domestic enquiry . 

Thon again , Shri Mohan claimed in the preliminary en 
quiry that he was in a position to identify the handwriting 
of Shri Şinha, thc concerned workman , in the mochanised 
attendance sheet for the period from April, 1983 to March - - 
May , 1984. But he did nothing of the soit in the Joms 
tic enquiry . That being so , the allegation of crasing out of 
the inflated figuros by the concerned workman has not been 
established to the bill. 

Shri Mohan stated that the concerned workman inflated 
the figure in respect of 52 and 26 daily rated employees 
with malafide intention and ulterior motive for his per onal 
gain . Tho Enquiry Ollicer also held that the conceined 
workmen willfully / deliberately with malafide intention for 
the purpose of personal gain booked inilated figurcs in the 
mechanised ation lance shcet in respect of 52 daily rated em 
ployees of Ergineering Departinent during thọ year 1983 -84 
and by this fraud the concerned workman caused excess 
paym nt to the tune of Rs. 1.49, 906 07. The e is not 
a whit of evidence that the concerned workman , by his 
allogod act of inflating figuro in mechanised attendance 
shçct he made any personrel gain or that he had an ul er 
ior inotive in doing so , On the contrary , some of the work 
men concerned why ieceived exceny payme - 1 wrte ex mined 
in ihm nreliminer cuiquiry and they did not inculcat the 
concerned workman . The management has subsequently re 
alised the excess amount paid to these workmen . This being 
so , I find no objective hasis to hold that the concerned 
workman allegedly committed any act of misconduct by in 
Hating tho figures of attendance of some of the daily rated 
workmen of Noonodih - Jitrus colliery from April , 1983 to 
April, 1984 for personal gain or with an ulterior motive .

S . Q . 1993.-- Whoroas the Central Governtuent is satisfied 
the public interost requiros that the Currency Noto Press,

मा विल्ली, 14 जुलाई, 1992

कि लोकहित में ऐसा करना अपेक्षित है, प्रौद्योगिक विवाद प्रधिनियम , 
1947 ( 1947 का 14 ) की धारा 2 के बंड ( ण ) की उपधारा ( 6 ) के 
के अनुसरण में भारत सरकार के श्रम मंत्रालय की तारीख 13 जनवरी,

sub -clause ( vi ) of clause ( n ) of section 2 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947 ) , the Central Government 
hercby declares with immediate effect the Currecy Note 
Press , Nashik Road to be a public utility service for the
